# Self-Healing Worker Pool with Capacity-Aware Scaling

## Context

Our SWE-bench parallel runner has a fundamental design flaw: at launch time, `evals/parallel.py` pins each worker to a specific API key from `auto_improve/api_keys.json`, distributed by index. When a worker's assigned key hits its rate limit (Z.AI weekly/monthly quota, transient 1305 service errors, or 429 rate limiting), the worker dies and stays dead — even when other healthy keys exist in the pool. Today we've shipped a launch-time key probe that filters dead keys upfront, but it doesn't solve the steady-state problem:

- A key that becomes exhausted mid-run still kills its owning worker permanently.
- The parent `run_parallel_workers` process hangs forever once all workers die — it never exits, so the cron's `restart_dead()` can't detect the condition via "process absent."
- Cron's `restart_dead()`, when it does fire, spawns a new parent without killing the old one, leaking orphan processes each cycle.
- Worker count is static — if the pool shrinks from 5 healthy keys to 2, we still try to run 4 workers and 2 of them thrash on dead keys.

Meanwhile, `megaplan/key_pool.py` already implements LRU rotation with cooldown INSIDE a single megaplan subprocess. The launch-time per-worker key pinning fights that — if the pin is removed, each megaplan phase call naturally rotates through healthy keys.

## Goal

Make the worker pool self-healing and capacity-aware. Two coordinated changes:

**Level 1: clean lifecycle.** Parent exits cleanly when all workers die. Cron kills the old parent before relaunching. Cron caches key probe results between runs so it can make informed scaling decisions without re-probing on every check.

**Level 2: shared-pool workers.** Remove the launch-time per-worker key pinning in `evals/parallel.py`. Worker subprocesses no longer receive a fixed key — they use the megaplan key pool's existing LRU+cooldown logic. Parent dynamically scales target worker count to `min(configured_max, alive_key_count)` at launch and re-scales if capacity changes significantly mid-run.

Explicit non-goal: we are NOT building a long-running key health daemon with RPC. The existing megaplan key pool (per-subprocess) plus cron-driven periodic probes (file-based state) is sufficient at the scale we operate. Keep the architecture simple.

## Project directory

`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent`

## What exists today (don't break)

- `evals/parallel.py` — `run_parallel_workers` and `join_parallel_run` already call `_probe_and_filter_keys` (just shipped in the key-probe-preflight plan) and scale worker count down based on live key probe results. `_launch_worker_processes` distributes keys to workers via the `api_keys[idx % len(api_keys)]` pattern, writing key + base_url into per-worker temp config files.
- `auto_improve/probe_keys.py` — probe module with `probe_all_keys`, `alive_keys`, `format_status_table`, `load_candidate_keys`, standalone CLI `python -m auto_improve.probe_keys`. Masks keys, handles Z.AI 429/401/timeout, returns a `KeyStatus` taxonomy. Already tested.
- `auto_improve/cron.py` — reads `_pidfile.json` for worker liveness (via pidfile, not pgrep), has `restart_dead()` which spawns a fresh `run_evals --config` when `procs["workers"] == 0`. Does NOT kill existing `loop_pid` before spawning.
- `megaplan/key_pool.py` — inside each megaplan subprocess, reads `api_keys.json` + env vars, handles LRU acquisition with per-key cooldowns (3600s for Z.AI quota errors). Do NOT touch this file. It already does the right thing per-subprocess; the bug is that the launch-time pinning defeats it.
- Worker subprocess config files (created by `evals/parallel.py`, stored in `/var/folders/...`): JSON with `workers=1`, `worker_id="worker-N"`, and currently `api_key` + `base_url` fields that pin the worker to a specific key.
- `_pidfile.json` in each iteration directory: has `loop_pid`, `workers[]` (per-worker dicts with `worker_id`, `pid`, `pgid`), `scorer_pid`.

## What needs to exist

### 1. Parent exits when all workers die

In `evals/parallel.py::run_parallel_workers` (around the main wait loop, roughly lines 200-300 — look for the worker subprocess tracking and wait logic), detect the condition "all launched worker subprocesses have exited (whether successfully or via failure)" and break the wait loop. The parent should then:

- Perform normal cleanup (close logs, update pidfile to reflect `loop_pid=None` or remove it, write a final summary)
- Exit with a non-zero status code so the cron can distinguish "all workers died" from "all workers completed successfully"
- Do NOT attempt to respawn workers from inside the parent (that's the cron's job in the current design, and the supervisor pattern is out of scope)

The parent should also handle the case where every worker hit the "no alive key available" path during startup — this should be treated the same as "all workers died," exit non-zero.

Write a test in `tests/test_parallel_preflight.py` (or a new file if cleaner) that mocks worker subprocesses that all exit immediately, asserts `run_parallel_workers` returns in under 5 seconds instead of hanging.

### 2. Cron kills the old parent before relaunching

In `auto_improve/cron.py::restart_dead`, when `procs["workers"] == 0` is detected AND `fix=True`:

- Before the `subprocess.Popen([...run_evals...])` call, read the current `_pidfile.json` and extract `loop_pid`.
- If `loop_pid` is set AND `_pid_alive(loop_pid)`: send SIGTERM to it, wait up to 5 seconds, then SIGKILL if still alive.
- Log what happened: "FIXED: killed orphan parent PID <N> before relaunch" or "FIXED: no orphan parent to clean up".
- Only after the old parent is dead, spawn the new one.

Add a corresponding test in `tests/test_cron.py` (create the file if it doesn't exist) that monkeypatches `_pgrep`, `_pid_alive`, `os.kill`, and `subprocess.Popen`, and asserts the old `loop_pid` is killed before the new Popen call.

### 3. Cached key status from the hourly cron

Add a new function in `auto_improve/cron.py` called `run_key_probe_cached(max_age_s: int = 300) -> list[KeyStatus]`:

- Reads `_cron_state.json` inside `ITER_DIR` (already exists; holds `scores`, `procs`, `stall_count`, etc).
- Looks for a `key_probe` field with `timestamp` and `results` (list of dicts matching KeyStatus).
- If the cached data is fresher than `max_age_s`, return the deserialized results.
- Otherwise: import `probe_all_keys` from `auto_improve.probe_keys`, run it, serialize results into the state file, return them.

Call this function once at the top of `cron.main()`. Use the results for:
- A new diagnostic block in the cron output showing "Keys: <alive>/<total> alive" and listing any non-alive keys with their status + reset time
- Input to a new `check_key_capacity()` function that warns if `num_alive < configured_workers` — "Capacity mismatch: N keys alive but M workers configured. Consider scaling." (advisory, not a fix).

Cron run time should increase by ≤5 seconds (the probe is fast). Verify in the test suite.

### 4. Remove launch-time per-worker key pinning

In `evals/parallel.py::_launch_worker_processes`:

- The per-worker temp config file currently writes `api_key` and `base_url` fields derived from `api_keys[idx % len(api_keys)]`. **Stop writing those fields** into the per-worker temp configs.
- Keep writing `worker_id`, `workers=1`, and everything else that's not key-specific.
- The `api_keys` parameter is still used for the launch-time probe (via `_probe_and_filter_keys`) to determine how many workers to launch — but the keys themselves are NOT passed to individual workers.

In `evals/run_evals.py` (or wherever the worker subprocess reads its config and sets up the API client): when the per-worker config has no `api_key` field, fall back to the megaplan key pool's normal resolution order — `api_keys.json` + env vars, LRU acquisition with cooldown. This is the existing behavior inside megaplan subprocesses; we just need to make sure nothing else was relying on the per-worker pinned key.

Search the repo for any code that reads `api_key` from the worker config and either update it to treat the field as optional or fall through to the pool. Pay specific attention to:
- `evals/run_evals.py` and anything it imports for the worker setup
- Any logging that prints "worker-N: key=..." — should log "worker-N: key=<pool>" or similar when no pin is set

Add a test in `tests/test_parallel_preflight.py` asserting that when `_launch_worker_processes` is called, the generated temp config for each worker does NOT contain `api_key` (use a tempdir and inspect the written JSON).

### 5. Dynamic worker scale-down based on cron's probe cache

In `auto_improve/cron.py`, add a new check `check_key_capacity(procs, cached_probe) -> list[str]`:

- If `len(alive_keys) < procs["workers"]`: flag an advisory issue "Over-provisioned: N workers running but only M alive keys. Consider killing N-M workers or waiting for recovery."
- If `len(alive_keys) > procs["workers"]` AND `procs["workers"] < _expected_workers()`: flag an advisory "Under-provisioned: M alive keys but only N workers running. Could scale up."
- This function is advisory only in v1 — it does NOT auto-kill or auto-spawn workers. The `restart_dead` path still handles the "all dead → relaunch" case, and a scaled relaunch will naturally match capacity via `_probe_and_filter_keys` at launch time.

Add the check to `cron.main()`. Test it in `tests/test_cron.py` with mocked alive counts.

### 6. Update the runbook

Edit `auto_improve/CRON_RUNBOOK.md`:

- Document the new self-healing behavior in a new "How self-healing works" section. Explain: workers don't own keys; parent exits when all workers die; cron detects via pidfile and relaunches with probe; new launch scales to current alive-key count.
- Add a note under "Zero-progress stall" explaining that if the cron reports "over-provisioned," the operator can safely kill N-M workers to reduce contention, and the next cron fire's relaunch will scale correctly if they all die.
- Add the `check_key_capacity` behavior to the known issues / advisory output section.

### 7. Tests

- `tests/test_parallel_preflight.py` — extend with:
  - "Parent exits when all workers die" (mocked subprocesses that exit immediately, assert parent returns in < 5s with non-zero code)
  - "Per-worker temp config has no api_key pin" (inspect the written JSON)
- `tests/test_cron.py` — create if needed, or extend existing cron tests:
  - "restart_dead kills old loop_pid before relaunch" (monkeypatched `_pid_alive`, `os.kill`, `subprocess.Popen`, assert call order)
  - "run_key_probe_cached respects max_age_s" (monkeypatched probe function, assert cache hit skips the probe call)
  - "check_key_capacity flags over-provisioned" (synthetic alive_keys vs procs values)
  - "cron.main calls run_key_probe_cached once" (monkeypatch and spy)
- Full regression: `pytest -m 'not integration'` should pass (acknowledging the ~320 pre-existing failures in unrelated ACP / permission tests — use `-k` to filter if needed, or make the pass criterion "no new failures introduced by these changes").

## Out of scope

- Level 3 daemon / RPC supervisor. Do NOT build `auto_improve/key_daemon.py` or `evals/supervisor.py`.
- Changes to `megaplan/key_pool.py`. The existing per-subprocess LRU+cooldown is sufficient.
- Mid-run dynamic scaling from within a running parent. Scale-ups/downs happen at relaunch time only.
- MiniMax probing integration into the cron cache (existing launch-time probe is zhipu-only; keep that scope).
- Changes to iteration-021 or iteration-022-robust data.
- Real-time cross-worker API call rate balancing.
- Replacing the existing `check_worker_quota` / `check_worker_staleness` functions in cron (they should coexist with the new capacity check).

## Success criteria

- Parent `run_parallel_workers` exits cleanly with a non-zero code when all workers die, within 5 seconds of the last worker exit. Unit test demonstrates this.
- Cron's `restart_dead` never leaks an orphan parent — old `loop_pid` is killed before a new one is spawned. Unit test demonstrates this.
- Cron output includes a "Keys: N/M alive" line showing cached probe results; cache is refreshed when older than 5 minutes.
- Worker subprocess temp configs no longer contain `api_key` or `base_url` fields. Unit test inspects the written JSON.
- Worker subprocesses use megaplan's shared key pool (via `api_keys.json` + env) for all API calls. A key that becomes exhausted mid-phase causes the megaplan pool's LRU+cooldown to rotate to a different key without killing the worker.
- Runbook documents the self-healing flow and the capacity diagnostic.
- All new tests pass; no new pre-existing test regressions introduced.
- Manual end-to-end validation: kill all workers manually in a running experiment, wait for the next cron fire, confirm (a) old parent gone, (b) new parent launched, (c) probe runs, (d) new workers spawned at current alive-key capacity, (e) no per-worker key pinning in their temp configs.

## Files likely to change

- `evals/parallel.py` — parent-exit logic, remove per-worker key pinning
- `evals/run_evals.py` — handle missing `api_key` in worker config (fall through to pool)
- `auto_improve/cron.py` — kill old parent in `restart_dead`, new `run_key_probe_cached`, new `check_key_capacity`
- `auto_improve/CRON_RUNBOOK.md` — document self-healing flow and new diagnostic
- `tests/test_parallel_preflight.py` — new test cases
- `tests/test_cron.py` — create or extend

# Implementation Plan: Self-Healing Worker Pool with Capacity-Aware Scaling

## Overview

Goal: make the SWE-bench parallel runner self-healing. Workers stop owning fixed API keys (each megaplan subprocess already does LRU + cooldown via `megaplan/key_pool.py`); the parent exits cleanly when every worker has died; cron kills the orphan parent before relaunching; and cron caches a periodic key probe so it can scale launches and surface advisories without re-probing every minute.

Repo state confirmed by inspection:

- `evals/parallel.py:200` already calls `_probe_and_filter_keys` and scales the launch count. Per-worker "pinning" today is **not** done via the worker temp config JSON (the brief was slightly inaccurate). It is done in `_launch_worker_processes` at `evals/parallel.py:612-633` by setting `worker_env["ZHIPU_API_KEY"]` and rewriting the worker's copy of `~/.hermes/.env`. `_write_worker_config` (`evals/parallel.py:734`) never writes `api_key`/`base_url`. `evals/run_evals.py` contains zero references to `api_key` or `ZHIPU_API_KEY`. The fix is therefore to delete the env-var override block, not to scrub config JSON.
- `_wait_for_workers` (`evals/parallel.py:694`) loops `while len(completed) < len(worker_procs)` then `time.sleep(10)`. When all workers exit, the loop terminates naturally — but `run_parallel_workers` then proceeds into `manifest_wait` (`evals/parallel.py:323`) which can hang for `MANIFEST_WAIT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`. We need an "all workers died" exit short-circuit *and* a non-zero return path that the cron can act on.
- `auto_improve/cron.py:386 restart_dead` spawns a new `run_evals` without ever killing `loop_pid`. `_pidfile.json` is read by `_read_iteration_pidfile()` (`auto_improve/cron.py:125`).
- `auto_improve/probe_keys.py` exposes `probe_all_keys`, `alive_keys`, `KeyStatus` (frozen dataclass with `asdict` already imported).
- `tests/test_cron.py` does not exist yet; `tests/test_parallel_preflight.py` does.

Constraints: don't touch `megaplan/key_pool.py`. Don't build a daemon. Keep cron added latency ≤ 5s. The advisory `check_key_capacity` is reporting-only — no auto kill/spawn.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Drop per-worker key pinning in `_launch_worker_processes` (`evals/parallel.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Delete** the env-var override block at `evals/parallel.py:612-633` (the `if api_keys: ... ZHIPU_API_KEY ... worker_env_file.write_text(...)` block, plus the `print(f"  {worker_id}: key=...")` line). Workers will inherit the parent's environment and resolve keys via `megaplan/key_pool.py` per subprocess.
2. **Replace** the per-worker print with a single launch-time line: `print(f"  {worker_id} started — keys via shared pool ({len(api_keys)} alive)", file=sys.stderr)` (after the `subprocess.Popen`).
3. **Keep** `api_keys` as a parameter — `_probe_and_filter_keys` still uses it to size the worker pool. Update the comment at `evals/parallel.py:578-581` from "Distributing N API keys across workers" to "N alive keys available; workers will draw from the shared pool".
4. **Verify** by grep that no other code path reads `ZHIPU_API_KEY` from the worker temp config (`Grep "api_key"` on `evals/run_evals.py` already returns zero — confirmed).

### Step 2: Parent exits cleanly when all workers die (`evals/parallel.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Modify** `_wait_for_workers` (`evals/parallel.py:694`) to also return an `all_failed: bool` (or to make `run_parallel_workers` derive it from the returned `completed` map). Simpler: have `run_parallel_workers` compute `all_failed = bool(completed) and all(rc != 0 for rc in completed.values())` after the wait call.
2. **Short-circuit** the manifest-wait loop at `evals/parallel.py:319-337`: if every worker exited non-zero, skip the wait, log `"All workers died — aborting parent"`, set a flag.
3. **Handle** the "no alive key at startup" case: in `run_parallel_workers`, after `_probe_and_filter_keys`, if `workers == 0`, log `"No alive keys — aborting"` and return immediately with non-zero status (treat as `all_failed`).
4. **Plumb** the non-zero exit through to the CLI: find the call site of `run_parallel_workers` (likely in `evals/run_evals.py` main) and `sys.exit(1)` (or 2) when the returned summary has `all_workers_failed=True`. Add that key to the summary dict in the all-died branch.
5. **Cleanup**: still run the existing `finally` block (`evals/parallel.py:364`) so `_clear_pidfile_runtime_state` and temp-config unlink fire. The atexit `_kill_workers` is harmless when procs are already dead.
6. **Bound** the wait: ensure `_wait_for_workers`'s 10-second sleep is short enough — it is. The all-dead detection happens within one poll cycle (≤10s); the success criterion is "<5s of last worker exit". Tighten the poll interval to 1s when the count of live workers is ≤1, OR change the loop to `time.sleep(min(10, 1))` always — pick **always 2s** for simplicity; this is unit-testable.

### Step 3: Cron kills orphan parent before relaunching (`auto_improve/cron.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Add** an inline helper inside `restart_dead` (or just inline the logic) right before the `subprocess.Popen([... run_evals ...])` call at `auto_improve/cron.py:413`:
   ```python
   pf = _read_iteration_pidfile()
   loop_pid = pf.get("loop_pid")
   if loop_pid and _pid_alive(loop_pid):
       os.kill(loop_pid, signal.SIGTERM)
       for _ in range(50):  # up to 5s
           if not _pid_alive(loop_pid):
               break
           time.sleep(0.1)
       if _pid_alive(loop_pid):
           os.kill(loop_pid, signal.SIGKILL)
       killed_msg = f"killed orphan parent PID {loop_pid}"
   else:
       killed_msg = "no orphan parent to clean up"
   ```
2. **Append** `killed_msg` to the existing `"FIXED: All workers dead — restarted"` line so the operator sees both actions.
3. **Only** apply this in the `if procs["workers"] == 0` branch — scorer/dashboard restarts already work.

### Step 4: Cached key probe in cron (`auto_improve/cron.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Add** `run_key_probe_cached(max_age_s: int = 300) -> list[KeyStatus]`:
   - Read `_load_state()`, look for `state["key_probe"]` = `{"timestamp": ISO, "results": [dict, ...]}`.
   - If `timestamp` is fresher than `max_age_s`, rehydrate via `KeyStatus(**d)` and return.
   - Else: `from auto_improve.probe_keys import probe_all_keys`, call it, write `state["key_probe"] = {"timestamp": now_iso, "results": [asdict(r) for r in results]}`, `_save_state(state)`, return results.
   - Wrap probe in `try/except` so a probe failure does not break the cron — return `[]` and log a warning.
2. **Call** at the top of `main()` after `prev_state = _load_state()`:
   ```python
   key_probe = run_key_probe_cached()
   ```
   Note: this re-reads/writes state. Reorder so `prev_state` is captured *before* the probe call (the existing delta logic depends on prev), and the probe writes through `_load_state()` again internally — acceptable since `prev_state` is only used as a snapshot.
3. **Print** a diagnostic block:
   ```
   Keys: <alive>/<total> alive
     <masked_key> [<status>] <detail>   (one line per non-alive key)
   ```
   Use `auto_improve.probe_keys.alive_keys` for the count and the existing `format_status_table` only if convenient — otherwise hand-format the non-alive lines (one entry per line with `reset_at` if present).
4. **State persistence interaction**: the existing `_save_state` at `auto_improve/cron.py:580` overwrites the whole dict. Update that final write to **preserve** `state.get("key_probe")` so the cache survives across runs:
   ```python
   new_state = {..., "key_probe": prev_state.get("key_probe")}
   ```
   And ensure `run_key_probe_cached` itself reads/writes the file directly so its update is not clobbered by the final write — easiest: have `run_key_probe_cached` mutate a passed-in `state` dict and let `main` do the final write. Choose this pattern: `run_key_probe_cached(state: dict, max_age_s: int = 300) -> list[KeyStatus]` mutates `state["key_probe"]` in place and returns the parsed results. `main` is the single writer.

### Step 5: Capacity advisory (`auto_improve/cron.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Add** `check_key_capacity(procs: dict, key_probe: list[KeyStatus]) -> list[str]`:
   - `n_alive = len([k for k in key_probe if k.status == "alive"])` (or use `alive_keys()`).
   - If `n_alive < procs["workers"]`: return `[f"Over-provisioned: {procs['workers']} workers running but only {n_alive} alive keys. Consider killing {procs['workers']-n_alive} workers."]`.
   - If `n_alive > procs["workers"]` and `procs["workers"] < _expected_workers()`: return `[f"Under-provisioned: {n_alive} alive keys but only {procs['workers']} workers running. Could scale up."]`.
   - Else `[]`.
   - If `key_probe` is empty (probe failed), return `[]` (no false alarms).
2. **Wire** into `main()` after `restart_dead`:
   ```python
   all_issues.extend(check_key_capacity(procs, key_probe))
   ```

### Step 6: Tests (`tests/test_parallel_preflight.py`, `tests/test_cron.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Extend** `tests/test_parallel_preflight.py`:
   - `test_launch_workers_no_key_pinning_in_env`: monkeypatch `subprocess.Popen` to capture `env=...`, monkeypatch `_load_api_keys` to return one fake key, call `_launch_worker_processes` with a temp dir, assert the captured env's `ZHIPU_API_KEY` equals the **inherited** parent value (not the rewritten fake), and the worker's `.env` file does not contain the fake key. Equivalent assertion: the env-rewrite branch is gone.
   - `test_run_parallel_workers_exits_when_all_workers_die`: monkeypatch `_launch_worker_processes` to return a list of `subprocess.Popen`-like fakes that all `.poll()` return non-zero immediately, monkeypatch `_probe_and_filter_keys` to return `(["fake-key"], 2)`, assert `run_parallel_workers` returns within 5s and the resulting summary dict has `all_workers_failed=True` (or whatever flag we choose).
   - `test_run_parallel_workers_exits_when_no_alive_keys`: monkeypatch `_probe_and_filter_keys` to return `([], 0)`, assert immediate non-zero return.
2. **Create** `tests/test_cron.py`:
   - `test_restart_dead_kills_old_loop_pid_before_relaunch`: monkeypatch `_read_iteration_pidfile` → `{"loop_pid": 1234}`, `_pid_alive` → `True` then `False`, `os.kill` (record calls), `subprocess.Popen` (record calls). Call `restart_dead({"workers":0,"scorers":1,"dashboard":1}, fix=True)`. Assert `os.kill(1234, SIGTERM)` was called **before** `Popen`.
   - `test_run_key_probe_cached_uses_cache_when_fresh`: pass `state={"key_probe": {"timestamp": <now>, "results": [<one alive dict>]}}` and a monkeypatched `probe_all_keys` that raises if called. Assert it returns one parsed `KeyStatus` and `probe_all_keys` was not called.
   - `test_run_key_probe_cached_refreshes_when_stale`: timestamp 10 minutes old; assert `probe_all_keys` is called and `state["key_probe"]["timestamp"]` is updated.
   - `test_check_key_capacity_over_provisioned`: pass `procs={"workers":4}` and 2 alive keys; assert one issue containing `"Over-provisioned"`.
   - `test_check_key_capacity_under_provisioned`: monkeypatch `_expected_workers` to return 5, `procs={"workers":2}`, 4 alive keys; assert one issue containing `"Under-provisioned"`.
   - `test_check_key_capacity_balanced`: 3 alive keys, 3 workers → empty list.
   - `test_main_calls_probe_once`: spy on `run_key_probe_cached`, run `cron.main()` with the rest of the cron mocked enough to not crash (or extract the orchestration into a thin wrapper if main is too tangled — prefer light monkeypatching of the heavy functions).
3. **Run** `pytest tests/test_parallel_preflight.py tests/test_cron.py -x` first; once green, run `pytest -m 'not integration' -q` and confirm no **new** failures vs the documented baseline.

### Step 7: Update runbook (`auto_improve/CRON_RUNBOOK.md`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Add** a new "How self-healing works" section explaining: workers no longer own keys; megaplan pool LRU+cooldown handles rotation; parent exits non-zero when every worker dies; cron's `restart_dead` kills `loop_pid` before relaunching; relaunch scales to current alive-key count via `_probe_and_filter_keys`.
2. **Add** under "Zero-progress stall": guidance that "over-provisioned" advisories are safe to act on by killing N-M workers; the next cron tick will relaunch at correct capacity.
3. **Document** the new `Keys: N/M alive` diagnostic and the `check_key_capacity` advisory in the diagnostics section.

## Execution Order

1. Step 1 (drop pinning) — pure deletion, lowest risk, unblocks the rest.
2. Step 2 (parent clean exit) — needs Step 1 first so the all-died code path is reachable in tests with mocked workers without env weirdness.
3. Step 3 (cron orphan kill) — independent of 1/2 in code, but only useful once parent actually exits, so land after 2.
4. Step 4 (probe cache) — independent; can land in parallel with 3.
5. Step 5 (capacity advisory) — depends on Step 4.
6. Step 6 (tests) — written alongside each step but run together at the end.
7. Step 7 (runbook) — last.

## Validation Order

1. Run new unit tests in `tests/test_parallel_preflight.py` and `tests/test_cron.py` directly (`pytest -x`).
2. Run `pytest -m 'not integration' -q` and diff failure list against the known ~320 pre-existing failures — pass criterion is "no new failures".
3. Manual smoke (info-only): in a scratch dir, invoke `python -m auto_improve.cron` and confirm the new `Keys:` line appears and cron run time stays ≤ +5s.
4. Manual end-to-end (info-only, listed in success criteria): kill all workers in a live experiment, wait one cron tick, observe the kill+relaunch+probe sequence.

Confirming we should delete the env-var block at `evals/parallel.py:612-633` instead of scrubbing the JSON (which already has no key fields)."
  ],
  "success_criteria": [
    {
      "criterion": "`run_parallel_workers` returns within 5 seconds with a non-zero exit/flag when all worker subprocesses exit non-zero immediately, demonstrated by a unit test in `tests/test_parallel_preflight.py`.",
      "priority": "must"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`run_parallel_workers` returns immediately with a non-zero exit/flag when `_probe_and_filter_keys` reports zero alive keys, demonstrated by a unit test.",
      "priority": "must"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`_launch_worker_processes` no longer sets `ZHIPU_API_KEY`/`ZHIPU_BASE_URL` per worker and no longer rewrites the worker's `.env` file; verified by a test that monkeypatches `subprocess.Popen` and inspects the captured env.",
      "priority": "must"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`auto_improve/cron.py::restart_dead` sends SIGTERM (and SIGKILL on timeout) to the existing `loop_pid` from `_pidfile.json` *before* invoking `subprocess.Popen` for the new parent; verified by a test asserting call order.",
      "priority": "must"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`run_key_probe_cached` returns cached results without calling `probe_all_keys` when the cached entry is fresher than `max_age_s`, and refreshes (and persists) when stale; both paths covered by unit tests.",
      "priority": "must"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`check_key_capacity` flags 'Over-provisioned' when alive keys < workers and 'Under-provisioned' when alive keys > workers and workers < expected; both branches covered by unit tests.",
      "priority": "must"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`cron.main()` prints a `Keys: N/M alive` diagnostic line on every run, with non-alive keys listed below.",
      "priority": "must"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`pytest tests/test_parallel_preflight.py tests/test_cron.py` passes.",
      "priority": "must"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Running `pytest -m 'not integration'` introduces no new failures vs. the pre-change baseline (the ~320 unrelated pre-existing failures may remain).",
      "priority": "must"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`auto_improve/CRON_RUNBOOK.md` gains a 'How self-healing works' section and documents the over/under-provisioned advisory.",
      "priority": "must"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Cron runtime increases by no more than ~5 seconds vs. before this change (the probe is only run when cache is stale).",
      "priority": "should"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Each new function (`run_key_probe_cached`, `check_key_capacity`) does one thing and stays under ~30 lines.",
      "priority": "should"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "No fallbacks, defensive type conversions, or legacy compatibility shims added beyond what is concretely necessary (e.g., probe-failure \u2192 empty list is the only graceful-degrade case).",
      "priority": "should"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Manual end-to-end: in a live iteration, `kill -9` all workers; one cron tick later, the old `loop_pid` is gone, a new parent is running, the cron output shows the kill message, the probe ran, and new workers were spawned at current alive-key capacity with no `ZHIPU_API_KEY` in their per-worker env beyond what the cron itself inherited.",
      "priority": "info"
    }
  ],
  "assumptions": [
    "Single non-zero exit code (e.g., 1) from `run_parallel_workers` is sufficient \u2014 cron only needs to know 'parent should be relaunched', which it already infers from `procs['workers'] == 0` via the pidfile, not from exit code.",
    "Probe failures yield an empty `key_probe` list and `check_key_capacity` is silenced; the existing key cache is preserved across the failure (`run_key_probe_cached` only updates state on success).",
    "The brief's reference to per-worker config JSON containing `api_key`/`base_url` is outdated; the actual fix is to delete the env-var override + .env rewriting block in `_launch_worker_processes`.",
    "Tightening `_wait_for_workers`'s poll interval from 10s to 2s is acceptable \u2014 negligible CPU impact and meets the <5s exit criterion.",
    "Cron's `_save_state` will preserve `key_probe` across the existing state-rewrite at the end of `main()`, by passing the state dict through the probe helper rather than two independent reads.",
    "Worker subprocesses inheriting the parent cron/launcher's env (with whatever `ZHIPU_API_KEY` happens to be set at parent launch) is fine because megaplan's `key_pool.py` reads `api_keys.json` first and only falls back to env \u2014 the inherited env is just one more candidate in the pool, not a hard pin.",
    "`tests/test_cron.py` will mock heavy I/O (probe, manifest, scores) so it can run as a unit test without filesystem fixtures."

    {
      "id": "DEBT-060",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the user requirement 'parse the response \u2014 try to extract json' against the revised step 1 implementation. the plan explicitly declines json extraction and keeps raw substring search instead, even though the saved `django__django-10973` critique trace shows a prose preamble followed by a fenced ```json block, so this requirement is still only partially covered.",

    {
      "id": "DEBT-073",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the new patch-extraction path against the user requirement that file changes be reliably extractable for scoring. the revised plan still has no deterministic repository-supported extraction hook; it falls back to instructing the model in the execute prompt to run `git diff > /hermes_output/model_patch.diff`, which means patch capture now depends on agent compliance rather than infrastructure and only partially addresses the requirement.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

    {
      "id": "DEBT-074",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the revised image-name section against the watch items about opaque `sweb.env.*` hashes and multiple cached images. the plan still treats `docker images` matching as a fallback without specifying a reliable instance-to-image mapping, so the 'use the exact same image scoring uses' requirement is still only partially covered when more than one `sweb.env.*` image exists locally.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "run-the-hermes-agent-inside-20260401-0647"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-100",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the revised scope notes and success criteria against the actual hermes worker contract. the plan still says gate is unchanged because it uses `response_format` and plan is unchanged because it outputs markdown, but the repository still gives gate a file tool in `megaplan/hermes_worker.py:51-62`, only calls `set_response_format()` when there are no toolsets in `megaplan/hermes_worker.py:180-182`, and still routes plan through the `plan.json` schema plus worker payload fields in `megaplan/workers.py:48-65` and `megaplan/handlers.py:594-635`. that means the repo evidence still does not support the plan\u2019s claimed exclusions, so the earlier scope/issue-hints concern is not actually closed.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-structured-output-20260401-1616"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-129",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the revised `--skip-scoring` flow against the current `auto_improve.loop` control flow and `auto_improve.score_experiment` contract. the plan still says `loop.py` should call `score_iteration()` after a `scoring_mode=\"none\"` run unless `--skip-score` is also set, but in the current repo `score_iteration()` requires either `_watch_scores.json` or `_scoring_logs`; without a concurrently running external scorer, that post-run normalization path would fail immediately. the revised plan therefore introduces a new operator requirement it does not fully account for.",
      "occurrence_count": 2,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-7-remaining-operational-20260403-0034"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-130",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the kill-command requirement against the revised scorer design. the idea allows one scorer process to watch multiple iterations at once, and step 5 registers that same scorer pid into each active iteration's pidfile; step 4 then kills `scorer_pid` for `--iteration nnn`. that means `kill --iteration 021` would also stop scoring for 022/023 if they share the same scorer process, so the per-iteration kill semantics are still only partially satisfied.",
      "occurrence_count": 2,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-7-remaining-operational-20260403-0034"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-133",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: conventions-1 remains open: review_checks_for_robustness(robustness) returns review_checks for heavy and () for non-heavy, collapsing standard and light into the same behavior. the critique pattern at checks.py:139-144 uses three tiers (heavy/standard/light). this is a minor inconsistency but doesn't affect correctness since standard mode uses the existing monolithic path unchanged.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "robust-review-phase"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-134",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: conventions-2 remains open: reviewcheckspec typeddict (plan phase 2 step 1) has 4 fields (id, question, guidance, category) but critiquecheckspec at checks.py:11-17 has 6 including default_severity and tier. the parallel_review.py prompts would need to fill in missing fields when constructing check payloads. minor inconsistency in type definitions across the two registries.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "robust-review-phase"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-135",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: verification-8 remains open: the plan's lazy-import pattern (imports inside 'if robustness == heavy and agent_type == hermes' block) is the correct approach but no test verifies that standard mode at runtime does not transitively import megaplan.review_checks or megaplan.prompts.parallel_review. if those modules gain top-level imports (e.g., for type hints using non-standard-library types), standard mode would transitively load them despite the lazy-import guard. the plan should specify using type_checking for any such imports.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "robust-review-phase"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-136",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the phase 5 parallel_review_rework_count and the existing prior_rework_count (from history) both increment on 'needs_rework' verdicts from review. after 2 parallel review passes that both return needs_rework: parallel_rework_count=2 (cap hit, force-proceed to done) and prior_rework_count=2. if the user manually re-runs review after force-proceed (bypassing the parallel path), prior_rework_count=3 on entry, so max_review_rework_cycles=3 would allow one more pass before its own cap triggers. this is acceptable worst-case behavior, but the interaction between the two independent caps is not explicitly documented in the plan.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "robust-review-phase"
      ]
    }
  ],

    {
      "id": "DEBT-079",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: checked the new docker-backend surface against supporting infrastructure. adding `skip_home_overlay` and `extra_run_args` requires updates not just in `tools/environments/docker.py` and `tools/terminal_tool.py`, but also in every caller that populates `container_config`, including `tools/file_tools.py:109-118` and the terminal recreation path at `tools/terminal_tool.py:960-967`; the revised plan does not list those supporting changes.",

    {
      "id": "DEBT-080",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: checked the prompt/plumbing story against the actual benchmark api. the revised plan now depends on docker-enabled prompt text and a host-mounted `/hermes_output`, but `read_prompt()` still has no access to `prepared`, `config`, or prebuild state, and the plan does not add the necessary benchmark or run-loop glue to pass that information in before prompt generation.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "run-the-hermes-agent-inside-20260401-0647"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-145",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: phase 5 modification to _resolve_review_outcome adds a parallel-review-specific 2-iteration cap that, when triggered, force-proceeds to state_done bypassing the gate. the plan's own constraint states rework items must 'always through the gate for deadlock safety', yet the parallel cap returns success \u2192 state_done (skipping state_finalized \u2192 execute). if both standard-mode task_verdicts and parallel-review rework_items are pending simultaneously, hitting the parallel cap would force-proceed to done while standard rework is still unresolved. the plan does not account for this mixed-workload scenario. this is a known trade-off (2-iteration cap prevents infinite loops) but violates the stated gate-routing constraint.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "robust-review-phase"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-146",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: existing test suite in test_megaplan.py uses plan_fixture with robustness='standard' throughout and will not exercise the new heavy-mode parallel review branch. no existing test validates that handle_review in heavy mode correctly runs mechanical pre-checks and the 4 llm checks before the monolithic review (which is replaced). this means the most critical execution path has no regression coverage. phase 7 creates new test files, but existing test_megaplan.py tests remain on the standard path only.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "robust-review-phase"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "is-the-scope-appropriate": [

      "id": "DEBT-042",
      "concern": "is the scope appropriate?: flagged. the plan is under-scoped for the requested data-model change. `update_flags_after_critique()` currently only understands `check['finding']` and appends into `critique['flags']` at `megaplan/handlers.py:268-280`; `handle_critique()` still counts and summarizes `flags` at `megaplan/handlers.py:924,958`; `compute_recurring_critiques()` still compares `flags` only at `megaplan/evaluation.py:253-256`; and `build_gate_signals()` does not currently carry critique checks at `megaplan/evaluation.py:507-530`. supporting `findings[]`, `additional_concerns`, and full gate visibility needs broader handler/evaluation changes than the plan budgets.",

  ],
  "is-there-convincing-verification-for-the-change": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-062",
      "concern": "is there convincing verification for the change?: the verification story is still incomplete for the parser itself. the dry run verifies field stripping, and the live run verifies that responses come back, but there is still no planned repo-local check that the keyword matcher behaves correctly on the actual saved response shape already present in the traces, including prose preambles and fenced json. that leaves the most judgment-sensitive part of the script to manual eyeballing after the fact.",

      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-084",
      "concern": "is there convincing verification for the change?: checked the revised test plan against the new failure modes it introduces. the added tests would cover env override generation and some patch-from-file behavior, but they still do not exercise the critical paths where `read_prompt()` ordering, patch-only worker prebuild, file-tools-first sandbox creation, and prompt-driven patch emission could fail, so verification remains incomplete for the highest-risk branches.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "run-the-hermes-agent-inside-20260401-0647"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-085",
      "concern": "is there convincing verification for the change?: checked whether the must-have patch extraction behavior is backed by deterministic automation. it is not: the plan's core extraction path still depends on the model writing `model_patch.diff`, and no proposed repo-local test proves the system recovers when the model omits or corrupts that file.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "run-the-hermes-agent-inside-20260401-0647"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-105",
      "concern": "is there convincing verification for the change?: checked the revised test plan against the actual validator behavior. step 7 says a placeholder-only file should fail the required-key check and fall through, but the repo\u2019s generic validation only checks key existence in `megaplan/workers.py:221-227`, and the proposed critique/finalize/review templates would already have those keys. that means the planned placeholder test is based on a false premise and would not cover the real regression where an untouched template preempts a valid text response.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-structured-output-20260401-1616"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-122",
      "concern": "is there convincing verification for the change?: checked `tests/test_megaplan.py:1331-1365` and the revised test list. the verification story is still incomplete because the repo currently tests verified and disputed flag ids separately, but the plan does not add a merge-conflict test where the same flag is returned in both lists, and it also does not add a parity test for the simplified direct-aiagent parser against the current hermes fallback chain in `megaplan/hermes_worker.py:260-397`; those are the new failure modes most likely to slip through.",

  "key-source-resolution": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-156",
      "concern": "key source resolution: the plan does not match the actual key-pool source-of-truth. it only scans `~/.hermes/.env` for `zhipu_api_key`/numbered variants, then later proposes probing only the json subset for `evals/parallel.py`. in the real runtime, the zhipu pool unions `os.environ` and `~/.hermes/.env`, honors `glm_api_key` aliases, and then appends `auto_improve/api_keys.json`. as written, healthy env-only or alias-backed keys can be missed, causing false zero-key aborts or unnecessary worker scale-downs.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "key-probe-preflight"
      ]
    }

  "scoring-orchestration": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-069",
      "concern": "scoring orchestration: `auto_improve.run_experiment` already launches per-iteration watch scoring via `run_parallel_workers(..., scoring_mode=\"watch\")`, and `evals.parallel` starts `watch_and_score(results_root)` in a background thread. the plan adds `evals.watch_scoring_all` but does not disable or reroute that path, so the supported auto-improve flow can end up with two scorers hitting the same iteration, duplicating swe-bench runs and racing on `_watch_scores.json`.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "add-a-unified-scorer-that-20260401-0554"
      ]

  "search-for-related-code-that-handles-the-same-concept-is-the-reported-issue-a-symptom-of-something-broader": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-077",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: checked all active swe-bench scoring entry points again. `evals/watch_scoring.py:205-223` still hardcodes `--dataset_name princeton-nlp/swe-bench_verified`, while `evals/benchmarks/swe_bench.py` and `evals/parallel.py:547-556` still omit dataset selection, and the revised plan still does not budget changes for those paths even though the stated goal is 'use the exact same image scoring uses'.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "run-the-hermes-agent-inside-20260401-0647"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-078",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: checked the revised prebuild placement against the actual worker flow. moving prebuild out of `prepare_workspace()` avoids `--prepare-eval`, but patch-only worker subprocesses still run `run_all_evals()` after `evals/parallel.py:459-463` sets `swebench_patch_only=true`, so the plan's claim that patch-only workers avoid prebuild is not true unless it also guards on patch-only mode.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "run-the-hermes-agent-inside-20260401-0647"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-103",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: re-checked every hermes phase that uses structured output. beyond `prep`, `critique`, `finalize`, and `review`, the same tool-plus-schema pattern still exists for `plan`, `research`, `revise`, and `gate`: toolsets are assigned in `megaplan/hermes_worker.py:51-62`, schemas are defined in `megaplan/workers.py:48-59`, and the handlers persist those worker payloads in `megaplan/handlers.py:594-780`. the revised plan intentionally keeps those phases out of scope, but that means the broader repository-level reliability problem is still not fully contained.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-structured-output-20260401-1616"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-121",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: checked megaplan/workers.py:1022-1058, megaplan/handlers.py:679-735, megaplan/_core/state.py:95-108, and megaplan/workers.py:748-780. critique execution is not hermes-only today; it goes through generic worker dispatch, agent fallback, and one persisted `*_critic` session slot, so routing `handle_critique()` straight to `run_parallel_critique()` and `run_hermes_step()` would broaden the change into agent-routing and session-state behavior that the plan does not currently budget.",
      "occurrence_count": 2,
      "plan_ids": [
        "parallelize-the-critique-20260401-1840"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-125",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: checked `megaplan/workers.py:748-783`, `megaplan/workers.py:960-1021`, and `megaplan/_core/state.py:95-108`. the revised plan fixes the earlier agent-routing problem by gating parallelism to hermes, but it still changes critique session behavior more broadly than the current repo does: `resolve_agent_mode()` defaults critique to persistent mode, while the plan assumes `fresh=true` for all sub-agents and then records the overall parallel step as `refreshed=true`, so hermes critique would stop reusing the normal `hermes_critic` session at all unless the implementation adds an explicit replacement contract.",
      "occurrence_count": 2,
      "plan_ids": [
        "parallelize-the-critique-20260401-1840"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-128",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: checked the broader lifecycle model around item #2 and the revised plan still does not fully contain it. the repo now needs to coordinate three process shapes: the top-level loop, per-worker process groups, and a single scorer process that may span several iterations; storing one shared `scorer_pid` inside multiple iteration-local pidfiles means the process-management problem is still broader than a per-iteration pidfile kill story.",
      "occurrence_count": 2,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-7-remaining-operational-20260403-0034"
      ]
    }
  ],

      {
        "id": "DEBT-137",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: phase 1: the proposed `run_parallel_checks` signature includes `prompt_builder_fn` and `template_writer_fn` but omits the `run_check_fn: callable` callback that the plan text describes as the mechanism for step-specific behavior (agent creation, 429 fallback, parse logic). the critique-specific `_run_check` at parallel_critique.py:40-173 contains logic that cannot be parameterized through prompt/template builders alone \u2014 model resolution, minimax/openrouter 429 fallback chain, `parse_agent_output`, and `clean_parsed_payload`. without a `run_check_fn` callback in the signature, the generic primitive cannot correctly handle review's different parse/cleanup needs (review uses different schema and different payload structure than critique).",

        ]

      {
        "id": "DEBT-138",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: phase 4: `_merge_review_verdicts` (handlers.py:1017-1062) only merges `task_verdicts` and `sense_check_verdicts` into `finalize_data` by matching `task_id`/`sense_check_id`. it does not read or merge `review_checks` or `mechanical_prechecks` \u2014 these arbitrary payload fields would be stored in `worker.payload` and written to `review.json` but would not influence the verdict-count computation that drives `_resolve_review_outcome`. the plan's phase 4 step 6 says parallel findings are merged before `_merge_review_verdicts`, but the function has no provision for these fields.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "robust-review-phase"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-139",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: phase 4: mechanical pre-check failures are added only to `worker.payload['issues']` (via `extend`), not to `rework_items`. `_resolve_review_outcome` (handlers.py:1065-1102) determines `needs_rework` based only on `review_verdict`, `verdict_count`, `total_tasks`, `check_count`, `total_checks`, and `missing_evidence` \u2014 it does not inspect `issues`. this means a failing mechanical pre-check (e.g., `source_touch` finding no source files modified) would appear in `review.json` but would not automatically trigger the `needs_rework \u2192 execute` rework flow unless the llm review independently decides to set `review_verdict = 'needs_rework'`. the plan provides no guaranteed path for mechanical failures to trigger rework.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "robust-review-phase"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-140",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: phase 3: `diff_size_sanity` is described as comparing 'diff line count to the issue description length as a proxy', but issue text length in characters has no validated correlation with expected diff size in lines. the plan provides no concrete threshold formula (e.g., flag if `added_lines > 10 * (issue_chars / 100)` or a fixed threshold). without a specific heuristic, this check would produce unreliable pass/fail results.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "robust-review-phase"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-141",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: phase 2: `dead_guard` is described as a 'best-effort heuristic (not a full ast analysis)' using grep to find new `if`/`try` blocks and checking whether the enclosing function appears in at least one call site. this approach cannot determine reachability \u2014 a function appearing in a call site does not mean the specific new guard branch is reachable from that call. the plan's own framing ('best-effort') acknowledges this is unreliable, but the pass/fail result would be treated as authoritative in the review phase.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "robust-review-phase"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-142",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: phase 1: the plan text (step 4) says to create `runcheckcallable = callable[[int, dict, ...], tuple]` as a type alias, implying a callback pattern. however the concrete signature shown in step 1 omits `run_check_fn`, and phase 4 shows `run_parallel_checks` being called with only `prompt_builder_fn` and `template_writer_fn`. the review-specific per-thread callback (agent creation, model resolution, parse) is not shown being passed to the generic primitive, suggesting the plan does not fully specify how review's step-specific logic integrates with `run_parallel_checks`.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "robust-review-phase"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-143",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: phase 5: the plan adds a `parallel_review_originated` flag to history entries but `append_history` and `make_history_entry` in handlers.py do not currently accept this field. adding it requires modifying the history entry construction, which is shared by all step handlers \u2014 this is a broader change than phase 5 budgets for.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,

        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-144",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: phase 4: the plan says 'each check runs in its own fresh session via threadpoolexecutor \u2014 no cross-contamination', but `run_step_with_worker` (workers.py:1084) is called after the parallel checks and returns a fresh `workerresult`. the plan does not specify whether the parallel check findings are visible to the llm that runs in `run_step_with_worker`. if the llm review does not see the parallel findings in its prompt context, it could reach a different verdict than the parallel checks warrant.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "robust-review-phase"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-155",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: phase 5: when the parallel-review-specific 2-iteration cap is hit (parallel_rework_count >= 2), the code falls through to 'return success, state_done, none' at plan l549, bypassing the gate entirely. the task requirement states rework 'must flow through the existing gate loop' for deadlock safety, but the parallel cap forces directly to done. this is verified by tracing the control flow: l526 (parallel cap check) \u2192 l547 (gate path) \u2192 but l526 returns and falls through to l549. the comment at plan l531 ('fall through to success/done \u2014 still goes through gate for deadlock safety') is incorrect \u2014 the code goes straight to state_done without visiting the gate. while this is architecturally consistent with the existing max_review_rework_cycles cap at handlers.py:l1094-1098 (which also force-proceeds to done without gate), the plan's own comment mischaracterizes the behavior. the task constraint 'always through the gate for deadlock safety' is not satisfied by the parallel cap implementation. this is a trade-off: the parallel cap prevents infinite parallel-review loops but does so by bypassing the gate loop that provides deadlock safety. flag: the plan introduces a second force-proceed-to-done exit path (the first being the existing max_review_rework_cycles cap), both of which skip gate. this increases the paths that circumvent gate-based deadlock prevention. the plan's comment claiming gate-safety is factually wrong for the parallel cap branch.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "robust-review-phase"
        ]
      }

        Debt guidance:
        - These are known accepted limitations. Do not re-flag them unless the current plan makes them worse, broadens them, or fails to contain them.
        - Prefix every new concern with a subsystem tag followed by a colon, for example `Timeout recovery: retry backoff remains brittle`.
        - When a concern is recurring debt that still needs to be flagged, prefix it with `Recurring debt:` after the subsystem tag, for example `Timeout recovery: Recurring debt: retry backoff remains brittle`.

        Your output template is at: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/.megaplan/plans/self-healing-worker-pool/critique_output.json
Read this file first — it contains 5 checks, each with a question and guidance.
For each check, investigate the codebase, then add your findings to the `findings` array for that check.

Each finding needs:
- "detail": what you specifically checked and what you found (at least a full sentence)
- "flagged": true if this describes a difference, risk, or tension — even if you think it's justified. false only if purely informational with no possible downside.

When in doubt, flag it — the gate can accept tradeoffs, but it can't act on findings it never sees.

Good: {"detail": "Checked callers of nthroot_mod in solveset.py line 1205 — passes prime moduli only, consistent with the fix.", "flagged": false}
Good: {"detail": "The fix handles empty tuples but not single-element tuples which need a trailing comma.", "flagged": true}
Bad: {"detail": "No issue found", "flagged": false}  ← too brief, will be rejected
Bad: {"detail": "The hints suggest approach X but the plan uses Y. However Y is consistent with X's intent.", "flagged": false}  ← a different approach than the hints IS a flag. You found a divergence — flag it. The gate decides if it's acceptable.

After filling in checks, add any additional concerns to the `flags` array (e.g., security, performance, dependencies).
Use the standard format (id, concern, category, severity_hint, evidence). This array can be empty.

Workflow: read the file → investigate → read file again → add finding → write file back. Repeat for each check.

        Additional guidelines:
        - Robustness level: standard. Use balanced judgment. Flag significant risks, but do not spend flags on minor polish or executor-obvious boilerplate.
        - Over-engineering: prefer the simplest approach that fully solves the problem.
        - Reuse existing flag IDs when the same concern is still open.
        - `verified_flag_ids`: list flag IDs from prior iterations that the revised plan actually resolves (e.g., if the plan was revised to fix FLAG-001, and you confirm the fix is correct, include "FLAG-001"). Only include flags you've verified — don't guess.
        - Verify that the plan follows the expected structure when validator warnings or the outline suggest drift.
        - Additional flags may use these categories: correctness, security, completeness, performance, maintainability, other.
        - Focus on concrete issues, not structural formatting.

The plan’s correction is accurate: the worker JSON config does not currently contain `api_key` or `base_url`; the real launch-time pinning happens in `_launch_worker_processes()` by setting `ZHIPU_API_KEY`/`ZHIPU_BASE_URL` and rewriting the copied worker `.env` at `evals/parallel.py:612-633`.","flagged":false},{"detail":"Checked the requested cron helper contract against `auto_improve/cron.py`. The brief explicitly asks for `run_key_probe_cached(max_age_s: int = 300)`, but the plan changes that into a state-mutating helper that takes a `state` dict; that would work mechanically, but it is still a divergence from the user-specified interface and would force tests and documentation to follow a different API than requested.","flagged":true}]},{"id":"correctness","question":"Are the proposed changes technically correct?","findings":[{"detail":"Checked the planned 'all workers died' detection against actual worker exit behavior. `run_parallel_workers()` waits on `_wait_for_workers()` at `evals/parallel.py:253`, but `evals/run_evals.py::main()` always returns `0` after printing the summary at `evals/run_evals.py:1778-1789`, and manifest-driven task failures are handled inside `run_all_evals()` by marking manifest entries at `evals/run_evals.py:1103-1108` rather than by failing the process. Because of that, all workers can exit with code 0 while the manifest still has unfinished work, so the plan’s proposed `all(rc != 0 for rc in completed.values())` check would miss the hang it is supposed to fix.","flagged":true},{"detail":"Checked the startup zero-capacity path in `_probe_and_filter_keys()`. When no alive entries remain, the helper raises `RuntimeError` immediately at `evals/parallel.py:530-531`; it never returns `(alive_entries, 0)`. The plan’s branch 'after `_probe_and_filter_keys`, if `workers == 0`' is therefore unreachable unless the helper contract is changed too.","flagged":true},{"detail":"Checked the cached-probe scaling assumption against the current probing and launch code. `probe_all_keys()` uses `load_candidate_keys()` from `api_keys.json` plus `~/.hermes/.env` at `auto_improve/probe_keys.py:95-114`, but relaunch sizing in `run_parallel_workers()` and `join_parallel_run()` still comes from `_load_api_keys()` JSON-only at `evals/parallel.py:200-201` and `evals/parallel.py:418-419`. If cron starts using cached probe results for capacity advisories while launch-time scaling still ignores env-only keys, the reported healthy capacity can differ from the number of workers the parent will actually launch.","flagged":true}]},{"id":"scope","question":"Search for related code that handles the same concept. Is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?","findings":[{"detail":"Searched for other worker-launch paths and found `auto_improve/add_workers.py` still builds fixed `(worker_id, api_key, base_url)` assignments, sets `ZHIPU_API_KEY`/`ZHIPU_BASE_URL`, and rewrites each worker’s copied `.env` at `auto_improve/add_workers.py:54-159`. The plan only updates `evals/parallel.py`, so the repository would still keep another active entry point that reintroduces the exact fixed-key ownership model the change is trying to remove.","flagged":true},{"detail":"The broader capacity issue also includes the probe model itself, not just parent/cron lifecycle. `auto_improve/probe_keys.py` strips `api_keys.json` entries down to bare keys in `_load_api_keys_json()` and probes a single hard-coded `ZHIPU_ENDPOINT` at `auto_improve/probe_keys.py:52-68` and `auto_improve/probe_keys.py:117-135`, so a plan that wants cron’s cached probe to guide scaling is implicitly relying on a broader pool-health model than the repository currently stores.","flagged":true}]},{"id":"all_locations","question":"Does the change touch all locations AND supporting infrastructure?","findings":[{"detail":"Checked supporting integration points and the plan does identify the necessary process-level caller update in `evals/run_evals.py`, which is required because `main()` currently prints the parallel summary and returns 0 unconditionally at `evals/run_evals.py:1778-1789`. That part of the wiring is correctly called out.","flagged":false},{"detail":"Checked for duplicate infrastructure and found `auto_improve/add_workers.py` duplicates worker config writing, environment construction, and pidfile registration outside `evals.parallel.py` at `auto_improve/add_workers.py:98-166`. Without updating that script, the self-healing/shared-pool behavior would only be partial across the repo’s worker-management tooling.","flagged":true},{"detail":"Checked the probe/cache path against supporting infrastructure and `auto_improve/probe_keys.py` would need follow-on changes if cron output is expected to reflect the same usable pool as worker launches. Right now it does not preserve per-entry `base_url` from `api_keys.json` and only reads numbered `ZHIPU_API_KEY[_N]` values from `~/.hermes/.env`, so the cached `KeyStatus` data is not yet a full-fidelity representation of the worker pool described in the plan.","flagged":true}]},{"id":"callers","question":"Find the callers of the changed function. What arguments do they actually pass? Does the fix handle all of them?","findings":[{"detail":"`_probe_and_filter_keys()` has two real callers: `run_parallel_workers()` at `evals/parallel.py:200-201` and `join_parallel_run()` at `evals/parallel.py:418-419`. If the implementation changes that helper so 'zero alive' becomes a returned `(…, 0)` result instead of a raised `RuntimeError`, the join path needs matching handling too; the plan only discusses the launch path.","flagged":true},{"detail":"`_wait_for_workers()` is also called from two sites, `run_parallel_workers()` and `join_parallel_run()` at `evals/parallel.py:253` and `evals/parallel.py:441`. The plan’s idea of deriving the new 'all workers died' behavior in `run_parallel_workers()` instead of changing `_wait_for_workers()`’s contract would contain the new semantics to the parent path and avoid surprising the join caller.","flagged":false},{"detail":"Checked the parent exit-code plumbing against the actual caller behavior. The only process-level caller is `evals/run_evals.py::main()`, and it currently prints the `run_parallel_workers()` summary and returns 0 unconditionally at `evals/run_evals.py:1778-1789`. The plan is right that this caller must be updated, but the proposed summary flag has to cover both the post-wait all-workers-gone case and the startup zero-key exception path, not just an `all_workers_failed` branch.","flagged":true}]}],"flags":[{"id":"DEBT-156","concern":"Key source resolution: Recurring debt: the plan makes cron's cached probe a capacity signal even though `probe_all_keys()` counts JSON plus `~/.hermes/.env` keys while launch-time worker sizing in `evals.parallel` still uses JSON only, so advisories and relaunch worker counts can diverge.","category":"correctness","severity_hint":"likely-significant","evidence":"`auto_improve/probe_keys.py:95-114` loads candidates from `api_keys.json` and `~/.hermes/.env`, while `evals/parallel.py:200-201` and `evals/parallel.py:418-419` still size workers from `_load_api_keys()` only."},{"id":"DEBT-157","concern":"Probe base-url handling: Recurring debt: the plan relies on cached probe results for scaling and advisories, but `auto_improve/probe_keys.py` strips `api_keys.json` entries to bare keys and probes a hard-coded Z.AI endpoint, so non-default `base_url` entries are not represented in the cache.","category":"correctness","severity_hint":"likely-significant","evidence":"`auto_improve/probe_keys.py:52-68` drops `base_url` from JSON entries, and `auto_improve/probe_keys.py:117-135` always probes `ZHIPU_ENDPOINT` rather than any per-key endpoint."},{"id":"FLAG-SELFHEAL-ADD-WORKERS","concern":"Worker pool entrypoints: `auto_improve/add_workers.py` still pins per-worker keys and rewrites worker `.env` files, so the repository would keep a second operational path that bypasses the shared-pool design even if `evals/parallel.py` is fixed.","category":"completeness","severity_hint":"likely-significant","evidence":"`auto_improve/add_workers.py:54-159` constructs `(worker_id, api_key, base_url)` assignments,
